\displaystyle\text{app}\circ\langle\llbracket j^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}_{\underline{\mathit{A}}}\rrbracket\circ\langle\rangle,\text{app}\circ\langle\Lambda\Big(\mathbf{S}_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(\Lambda^{-1}\llbracket j^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\underline{\mathit{A}}}\rrbracket\circ\langle\langle\rangle,\text{id}\rangle\circ\pi_{2})\circ t^{\mathbf{S}_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}_{\top,\mathbf{S}_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\Big)\circ\langle\rangle,\delta^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}^{\ell\sqsubseteq\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\pi_{2}\rangle\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt\circ\langle\langle\rangle,\text{id}_{\mathbf{S}_{\ell}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{app}\circ\langle\llbracket j^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}_{\underline{\mathit{A}}}\rrbracket\circ\langle\rangle,\text{app}\circ\langle\Lambda\Big(\mathbf{S}_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}k^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\pi_{2}\Big)\circ\langle\rangle,\delta^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}^{\ell\sqsubseteq\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\pi_{2}\rangle\rangle\circ\langle\langle\rangle,\text{id}_{\mathbf{S}_{\ell}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt[\text{By IH and strength}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{app}\circ\langle\llbracket j^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}_{\underline{\mathit{A}}}\rrbracket\circ\langle\rangle,\mathbf{S}_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}k^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\pi_{2}\circ(\text{id}\times(\delta^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}^{\ell\sqsubseteq\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\pi_{2}))\circ\langle\langle\rangle,\text{id}\rangle\rangle\circ\langle\langle\rangle,\text{id}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{app}\circ\langle\llbracket j^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}_{\underline{\mathit{A}}}\rrbracket\circ\langle\rangle,\mathbf{S}_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}k^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\delta^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}^{\ell\sqsubseteq\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\pi_{2}\circ\pi_{2}\circ\langle\langle\rangle,\text{id}\rangle\rangle\circ\langle\langle\rangle,\text{id}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{app}\circ\langle\llbracket j^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}_{\underline{\mathit{A}}}\rrbracket\circ\langle\rangle,\mathbf{S}_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}k^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\delta^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}^{\ell\sqsubseteq\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\pi_{2}\rangle\circ\langle\langle\rangle,\text{id}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{app}\circ(\llbracket j^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}_{\underline{\mathit{A}}}\rrbracket\times\text{id}_{\mathbf{S}_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket})\circ\langle\langle\rangle,\mathbf{S}_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}k^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\delta^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}^{\ell\sqsubseteq\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\pi_{2}\rangle\circ\langle\langle\rangle,\text{id}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Lambda^{-1}\llbracket j^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}_{\underline{\mathit{A}}}\rrbracket\circ\langle\langle\rangle,\text{id}_{\mathbf{S}_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\rangle\circ\pi_{2}\circ\langle\langle\rangle,\mathbf{S}_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}k^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\delta^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}^{\ell\sqsubseteq\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\pi_{2}\rangle\circ\langle\langle\rangle,\text{id}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle k^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}k^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\delta^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}^{\ell\sqsubseteq\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\hskip 3.0pt[\text{By IH}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

∎

Theorem G.10 (Theorem
9.5
).

Let
\mathcal{L}
be a bounded join-semilattice.

•

If
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:\mathit{A}
in GMCC(
\mathcal{L}
), then
\llbracket\overline{\mathit{a}}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket
.

•

If
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:\mathit{A}
in
\text{DCC}_{e}
(
\mathcal{L}
), then
\llbracket\underline{\mathit{a}}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket
.

Proof.

Follows by theorems
G.7
and
G.8
.
∎

Theorem G.11 (Theorem
9.6
).

If
\ell\sqsubseteq\mathit{A}
in
\text{DCC}_{e}
, then
(\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket,k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket})
is an
\mathbf{S}_{\ell}
-algebra.

Further, if
\ell\sqsubseteq\mathit{A}
and
\ell\sqsubseteq\mathit{B}
, then for any
f\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket)
,
f
is an
\mathbf{S}_{\ell}
-algebra morphism.

Hence, the full subcategory of
\mathds{C}{}
with
\text{Obj}:=\{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket\>|\>\ell\sqsubseteq\mathit{A}\}
is also a full subcategory of the Eilenberg-Moore category,
\mathds{C}{}^{\mathbf{S}_{\ell}}
.

Proof.

Let
\ell\sqsubseteq\mathit{A}
. We show that
(\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket,k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket})
is an
(\mathbf{S}_{\ell},\mathbf{S}^{\bot\sqsubseteq\ell}\circ\eta,\mu^{\ell,\ell})
-algebra.

We use the following shorthand:
\overline{\eta}_{\mathit{X}}:=\mathbf{S}^{\bot\sqsubseteq\ell}_{\mathit{X}}\circ\eta_{\mathit{X}}
and
\overline{\mu}_{X}:=\mu^{\ell,\ell}_{\mathit{X}}
, where
X\in\text{Obj}(\mathds{C})
.

We need to show:
k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\overline{\eta}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}=\text{id}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}
. This follows by lemma
G.1
.

Next, we need to show:
k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}_{\ell}k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}=k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\overline{\mu}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}
.

Now,

\displaystyle k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\overline{\eta}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle=\text{id}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle\text{or, }\mathbf{S}_{\ell}k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}_{\ell}\overline{\eta}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle=\text{id}_{\mathbf{S}_{\ell}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle\text{or, }k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}_{\ell}k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}_{\ell}\overline{\eta}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle=k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle\text{or, }k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}_{\ell}k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}_{\ell}\overline{\eta}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\overline{\mu}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle=k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\overline{\mu}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle\text{or, }k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}_{\ell}k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\delta^{\ell,\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mu^{\ell,\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}_{\ell}\overline{\eta}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\overline{\mu}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle=k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\overline{\mu}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle\text{or, }k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}_{\ell}k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\delta^{\ell,\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\overline{\mu}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}_{\ell}\overline{\eta}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\overline{\mu}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle=k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\overline{\mu}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle\text{or, }k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}_{\ell}k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\delta^{\ell,\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\overline{\mu}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle=k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\overline{\mu}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\hskip 3.0pt[\because\>(\mathbf{S}_{\ell},\overline{\eta},\overline{\mu})\text{ is a monad}]

\displaystyle\text{or, }k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}_{\ell}k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\delta^{\ell,\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mu^{\ell,\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle=k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\overline{\mu}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle\text{or, }k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}_{\ell}k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle=k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\overline{\mu}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

Hence,
(\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket,k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket})
is an
\mathbf{S}_{\ell}
-algebra.

Next, let
\ell\sqsubseteq\mathit{A}
and
\ell\sqsubseteq\mathit{B}
such that
f\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket)
.

We show that
f
is an
\mathbf{S}_{\ell}
-algebra morphism.

Need to show:
\mathit{f}\circ k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}=k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}_{\ell}\mathit{f}
.

Since
\overline{\eta}
is a natural transformation, we have:

\displaystyle\overline{\eta}_{\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\mathit{f}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{S}_{\ell}\mathit{f}\circ\overline{\eta}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle\text{or, }k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\overline{\eta}_{\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\mathit{f}\circ k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle=k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}_{\ell}\mathit{f}\circ\overline{\eta}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle\text{or, }\text{id}_{\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\mathit{f}\circ k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle=k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}_{\ell}\mathit{f}\circ\overline{\eta}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\hskip 3.0pt\text{[By Lemma \ref{Ap2}]}

\displaystyle\text{or, }\mathit{f}\circ k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle=k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}_{\ell}\mathit{f}\circ\text{id}_{\mathbf{S}_{\ell}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\hskip 3.0pt\text{[By Lemma \ref{Ap3}]}

\displaystyle\text{or, }\mathit{f}\circ k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle=k^{\ell}_{\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\mathbf{S}_{\ell}\mathit{f}

The final clause of the theorem follows.
∎

Theorem G.12 (Theorem
9.7
).

\llbracket\_\rrbracket_{(\mathbf{Set},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})}
, for any
\ell\in L
, is a computationally adequate interpretation of
\text{DCC}_{e}
(
\mathcal{L}
).

Proof.

For a bicartesian category
\mathds{C}
, any strong monoidal functor from
\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{L})
to
\mathbf{End}_{\mathds{C}}^{\text{s}}{}
provides a computationally adequate interpretation of
\text{DCC}_{e}
(
\mathcal{L}
), given the interpretation for ground types is injective. Now, with respect to
\llbracket\_\rrbracket_{(\mathbf{Set},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})}
, the interpretation for ground types is injective. As such, to prove that
(\mathbf{Set},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})
is a computationally adequate interpretation of
\text{DCC}_{e}
(
\mathcal{L}
), we just need to show that
\mathbf{S}^{\ell}
is a strong monoidal functor from
\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{L})
to
\mathbf{End}_{\mathds{C}}^{\text{s}}{}
.

Recall the definition of
\mathbf{S}^{\ell}
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}^{\ell}(\ell^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\textbf{Id},&\text{ if }\ell^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\ell\\
\ast,&\text{ otherwise }\end{cases}\mathbf{S}^{\ell}(\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\sqsubseteq\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\text{id},&\text{ if }\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}\sqsubseteq\ell\\
\langle\rangle,&\text{ otherwise }\end{cases}

By this definition,
\mathbf{S}^{\ell}({\bot})=\textbf{Id}{}
. Further,
\eta=\epsilon=\text{id}{}_{\textbf{Id}{}}
.

Now, for any
\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}\in L
, there are two cases to consider:

•

\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\sqsubseteq\ell
and
\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}\sqsubseteq\ell
. Then,
\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}\sqsubseteq\ell
.

So,
\mathbf{S}^{\ell}({\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}})=\mathbf{S}^{\ell}({\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}})=\mathbf{S}^{\ell}({\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}})=\textbf{Id}{}
.

In this case,
\mu^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=\delta^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=\text{id}{}_{\textbf{Id}{}}
.

•

\neg(\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\sqsubseteq\ell)
or
\neg(\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}\sqsubseteq\ell)
. Then,
\neg(\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}\sqsubseteq\ell)
.

So,
\mathbf{S}^{\ell}({\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}})=\ast
or
\mathbf{S}^{\ell}({\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}})=\ast
. Hence,
\mathbf{S}^{\ell}({\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\circ\mathbf{S}^{\ell}({\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}})=\ast
. Further,
\mathbf{S}^{\ell}({\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}})=\ast
.

In this case,
\mu^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=\delta^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=\text{id}{}_{\ast}
.

Hence,
\mathbf{S}^{\ell}
is a strong (in fact a strict) monoidal functor from
\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{L})
to
\mathbf{End}_{\mathds{C}}^{\text{s}}{}
.
∎

Lemma G.13 (Lemma
9.8
).

If
\ell^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathit{A}
and
\neg(\ell^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\ell)
, then
\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})}\cong\top
.

Proof.

By induction on
\ell^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathit{A}
.

•

Rule
Prot-Prod
. Have:
\ell^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathit{A}\times\mathit{B}
, where
\ell^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathit{A}
and
\ell^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathit{B}
. Further,
\neg(\ell^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\ell)
.

Need to show:
\llbracket\mathit{A}\times\mathit{B}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})}\cong\top
.

By IH,
\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})}\cong\top
and
\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})}\cong\top
.

Therefore,
\llbracket\mathit{A}\times\mathit{B}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})}=\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})}\times\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})}\cong\top\times\top\cong\top
.

•

Rule
Prot-Fun
. Have:
\ell^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B}
, where
\ell^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathit{B}
. Further,
\neg(\ell^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\ell)
.

Need to show:
\llbracket\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})}\cong\top
.

By IH,
\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})}\cong\top
.

Therefore,
\llbracket\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})}=(\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})})^{(\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})})}\cong\top^{(\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})})}\cong\top
.

•

Rule
Prot-Monad
. Have:
\ell^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{T}_{\ell^{\prime}}\>\mathit{A}
, where
\ell^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\ell^{\prime}
. Further,
\neg(\ell^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\ell)
.

Need to show:
\llbracket\mathcal{T}_{\ell^{\prime}}\>\mathit{A}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})}\cong\top
.

Now, since
\ell^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\ell^{\prime}
and
\neg(\ell^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\ell)
, so
\neg(\ell^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\ell)
.

Then,
\llbracket\mathcal{T}_{\ell^{\prime}}\>\mathit{A}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})}=(\mathbf{S}^{\ell}({\ell^{\prime}}))\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})}=(\ast)\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})}=\top
.

•

Rule
Prot-Already
. Have:
\ell^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{T}_{\ell^{\prime}}\>\mathit{A}
, where
\ell^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathit{A}
. Further
\neg(\ell^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\ell)
.

Need to show:
\llbracket\mathcal{T}_{\ell^{\prime}}\>\mathit{A}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})}\cong\top
.

By IH,
\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})}\cong\top
.

Therefore,
\llbracket\mathcal{T}_{\ell^{\prime}}\>\mathit{A}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})}=(\mathbf{S}^{\ell}({\ell^{\prime}}))\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})}\cong(\mathbf{S}^{\ell}({\ell^{\prime}}))(\top)\cong\top
.

•

Rule
Prot-Minimum
. Have:
\bot\sqsubseteq\mathit{A}
.

Further,
\neg(\bot\sqsubseteq\ell)
. But this is a contradiction because for any
\ell\in L
,
\bot\sqsubseteq\ell
.

•

Rule
Prot-Combine
. Have:
\ell^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathit{A}
, where
\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\sqsubseteq\mathit{A}
and
\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}\sqsubseteq\mathit{A}
and
\ell^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}
. Further,
\neg(\ell^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\ell)
.

Now, if both
\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\sqsubseteq\ell
and
\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}\sqsubseteq\ell
hold, then
\ell^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}\sqsubseteq\ell
.

Therefore, either
\neg(\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\sqsubseteq\ell)
or
\neg(\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}\sqsubseteq\ell)
.

In either case, by IH,
\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell})}\cong\top
.

∎

Theorem G.14 (Theorem
9.9
).

Let
\mathcal{L}=(L,\vee,\bot)
be the parametrizing semilattice.

•

Suppose
\ell\in L
such that
\neg(\ell\sqsubseteq\bot)
. Let
\ell\sqsubseteq\mathit{A}
. Let
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{f}:\mathit{A}\to\mathbf{Bool}
and
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{A}
and
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{A}
. Then,
\vdash\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\leadsto^{\ast}\mathit{v}
if and only if
\vdash\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\leadsto^{\ast}\mathit{v}
, where
\mathit{v}
is a value of type
\mathbf{Bool}
.

•

Suppose
\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in L
such that
\neg(\ell\sqsubseteq\ell^{\prime})
. Let
\ell\sqsubseteq\mathit{A}
. Let
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{f}:\mathit{A}\to\mathcal{T}_{\ell^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{Bool}
and
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{A}
and
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{A}
. Then,
\vdash\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\leadsto^{\ast}\mathit{v}
if and only if
\vdash\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\leadsto^{\ast}\mathit{v}
, where
\mathit{v}
is a value of type
\mathcal{T}_{\ell^{\prime}}\>\mathbf{Bool}
.

Proof.

For the first part, note that
(\mathbf{Set},\mathbf{S}^{\bot})
gives us a computationally adequate model of
\text{DCC}_{e}
(
\mathcal{L}
) (by Theorem
G.12
).

Next, since
\ell\sqsubseteq\mathit{A}
and
\neg(\ell\sqsubseteq\bot)
, by Lemma
G.13
,
\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\bot})}\cong\top
.

Therefore,
\llbracket\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket_{(\mathbf{Set},\mathbf{S}^{\bot})}=\llbracket\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket_{(\mathbf{Set},\mathbf{S}^{\bot})}=\text{app}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{f}\rrbracket_{(\mathbf{Set},\mathbf{S}^{\bot})},\langle\rangle\rangle
.

Then, by Theorem
G.6
,
\vdash\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\leadsto^{\ast}\mathit{v}
if and only if
\vdash\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\leadsto^{\ast}\mathit{v}
.

For the second part, note that
(\mathbf{Set},\mathbf{S}^{\ell^{\prime}})
gives us a computationally adequate model of
\text{DCC}_{e}
(
\mathcal{L}
) (by Theorem
G.12
). Further, note that
\overline{\eta}\triangleq(\mathbf{S}^{\ell^{\prime}}(\bot\sqsubseteq\ell^{\prime}))\circ\eta=\text{id}{}_{\textbf{Id}{}}\circ\text{id}{}_{\textbf{Id}{}}=\text{id}{}_{\textbf{Id}{}}
. So, the morphisms
\overline{\eta}_{\mathit{X}}
are mono for any
X\in\text{Obj}(\mathds{C})
.

Next, since
\ell\sqsubseteq\mathit{A}
and
\neg(\ell\sqsubseteq\ell^{\prime})
, by Lemma
G.13
,
\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{\ell^{\prime}})}\cong\top
.

Therefore,
\llbracket\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket_{(\mathbf{Set},\mathbf{S}^{\ell^{\prime}})}=\llbracket\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket_{(\mathbf{Set},\mathbf{S}^{\ell^{\prime}})}=\text{app}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{f}\rrbracket_{(\mathbf{Set},\mathbf{S}^{\ell^{\prime}})},\langle\rangle\rangle
.

Then, by Theorem
G.6
,
\vdash\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\leadsto^{\ast}\mathit{v}
if and only if
\vdash\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\leadsto^{\ast}\mathit{v}
.
∎

Appendix H
Proofs of lemmas/theorems stated in Section
10

Theorem H.1 (Theorem
10.1
).

If
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{n}\mathit{A}
in
\lambda^{\circ}
, then
\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,\mathbf{S}_{n}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket)
. Further, if
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{n}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{n}\mathit{A}
such that
\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\equiv\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
in
\lambda^{\circ}
, then
\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,\mathbf{S}_{n}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket)
.

Proof.

The first part follows by induction on the typing derivation. Most of the cases are straightforward, given the detailed interpretation in Section
10.2
. However, we elaborate on the case of
\mathbf{case}
-expressions.

•

Rule
LC-Case
. Have:
\Gamma\vdash\mathbf{case}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{n}\mathit{B}
where
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{n}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{n}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\to\mathit{B}
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{n}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\to\mathit{B}
.

Need to show:
\llbracket\mathbf{case}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,\mathbf{S}_{n}\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket)
.

By IH,
\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,\mathbf{S}_{n}(\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket))
and
\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,\mathbf{S}_{n}(\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket}))
and
\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,\mathbf{S}_{n}(\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}))
.

Now,

\displaystyle\llbracket\mathbf{case}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket=\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\langle\langle\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\rangle,\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket\rangle}(\mathbf{S}_{n}(\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket})\times\mathbf{S}_{n}(\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}))\times\mathbf{S}_{n}(\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket)\hskip 3.0pt[\text{By IH}]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{p^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket},\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}}\times\text{id}}\mathbf{S}_{n}(\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket}\times\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket})\times\mathbf{S}_{n}(\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{p^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket}\times\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket},\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}}\mathbf{S}_{n}((\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket}\times\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket})\times(\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathbf{S}_{n}(\mathit{h}\times\text{id})}\mathbf{S}_{n}(\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}\times(\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket))\xrightarrow{\mathbf{S}_{n}\text{app}}\mathbf{S}_{n}\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket

where
\mathit{h}=\Lambda\Big([\Lambda^{-1}\pi_{1}\circ\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{1}\rangle,\Lambda^{-1}\pi_{2}\circ\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{1}\rangle]\circ\Lambda^{-1}[\Lambda i_{1},\Lambda i_{2}]\circ\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{1}\rangle\Big)
.

Note that
\mathit{h}:\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket}\times\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}\to\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}
is an isomorphism, where

h^{-1}=\langle\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{i_{1}},\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{i_{2}}\rangle:\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}\to\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket}\times\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}

Recall that for
X,Y,Z\in\text{Obj}(\mathds{C})
and
f\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(Y,Z)
,

X^{f}\triangleq\Lambda\Big(X^{Z}\times Y\xrightarrow{\text{id}\times\mathit{f}}X^{Z}\times Z\xrightarrow{\text{app}}X\Big)\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(X^{Z},X^{Y})

For the second part, we invert the equality judgement.

•

(\lambda\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}.\mathit{b})\>\mathit{a}\equiv\mathit{b}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}
.

Now,

\displaystyle\llbracket(\lambda\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}.\mathit{b})\>\mathit{a}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{app}\circ\langle q_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\llbracket\lambda\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}.\mathit{b}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{app}\circ\langle q_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ q^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\Lambda\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{app}\circ\langle\Lambda\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket\rangle=\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket\circ\langle\text{id},\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket\rangle=\llbracket\mathit{b}\{\mathit{a}/\mathit{x}\}\rrbracket.

•

\mathit{b}\equiv\lambda\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}.\mathit{b}\>\mathit{x}
.

Now,

\displaystyle\llbracket\lambda\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}.\mathit{b}\>\mathit{x}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\Lambda\llbracket\mathit{b}\>\mathit{x}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\Lambda\Big(\text{app}\circ\langle q_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket\circ\pi_{1},\pi_{2}\rangle\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ q_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket.

•

\mathbf{prev}\>(\mathbf{next}\>\mathit{a})\equiv\mathit{a}
.

Now,
\llbracket\mathbf{prev}\>(\mathbf{next}\>\mathit{a})\rrbracket=\mu^{n,0^{\prime}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\delta^{n,0^{\prime}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket
.

•

\mathbf{next}\>(\mathbf{prev}\>\mathit{a})\equiv\mathit{a}
.

\llbracket\mathbf{next}\>(\mathbf{prev}\>\mathit{a})\rrbracket=\delta^{n,0^{\prime}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mu^{n,0^{\prime}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket
.

•

\mathbf{case}\>\mathbf{inj}_{1}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\equiv\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
.

Now,

\displaystyle\llbracket\mathbf{case}\>\mathbf{inj}_{1}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>\mathbf{of}\>\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>;\>\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{n}\text{app}\circ\mathbf{S}_{n}(\mathit{h}\times\text{id})\circ p^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket}\times\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket},\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}\circ p^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket},\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}}\times\text{id}\circ\langle\langle\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\rangle,\mathbf{S}_{n}i_{1}\circ\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{n}([\Lambda^{-1}\pi_{1}\circ\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{1}\rangle,\Lambda^{-1}\pi_{2}\circ\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{1}\rangle]\circ\Lambda^{-1}[\Lambda i_{1},\Lambda i_{2}]\circ\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{1}\rangle)\circ p^{-1}\circ p^{-1}\times\text{id}

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt\circ\langle\langle\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\rangle,\mathbf{S}_{n}i_{1}\circ\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{n}([\Lambda^{-1}\pi_{1}\circ\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{1}\rangle,\Lambda^{-1}\pi_{2}\circ\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{1}\rangle]\circ\Lambda^{-1}[\Lambda i_{1},\Lambda i_{2}])\circ p^{-1}\circ\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{1}\rangle\circ p^{-1}\times\text{id}

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt\circ\langle\langle\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\rangle,\mathbf{S}_{n}i_{1}\circ\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{n}([\Lambda^{-1}\pi_{1}\circ\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{1}\rangle,\Lambda^{-1}\pi_{2}\circ\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{1}\rangle]\circ\Lambda^{-1}[\Lambda i_{1},\Lambda i_{2}])\circ p^{-1}\circ\langle\mathbf{S}_{n}i_{1}\circ\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,p^{-1}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\rangle\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{n}([\Lambda^{-1}\pi_{1}\circ\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{1}\rangle,\Lambda^{-1}\pi_{2}\circ\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{1}\rangle]\circ\Lambda^{-1}[\Lambda i_{1},\Lambda i_{2}])\circ p^{-1}\circ\mathbf{S}_{n}i_{1}\times\text{id}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,p^{-1}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\rangle\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{n}([\Lambda^{-1}\pi_{1}\circ\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{1}\rangle,\Lambda^{-1}\pi_{2}\circ\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{1}\rangle]\circ\Lambda^{-1}[\Lambda i_{1},\Lambda i_{2}])\circ\mathbf{S}_{n}(i_{1}\times\text{id})\circ p^{-1}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,p^{-1}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\rangle\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{n}[\Lambda^{-1}\pi_{1}\circ\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{1}\rangle,\Lambda^{-1}\pi_{2}\circ\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{1}\rangle]\circ\mathbf{S}_{n}\Lambda^{-1}[\Lambda i_{1},\Lambda i_{2}]\circ\mathbf{S}_{n}(i_{1}\times\text{id})\circ p^{-1}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,p^{-1}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\rangle\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{n}[\Lambda^{-1}\pi_{1}\circ\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{1}\rangle,\Lambda^{-1}\pi_{2}\circ\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{1}\rangle]\circ\mathbf{S}_{n}i_{1}\circ p^{-1}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,p^{-1}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\rangle\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{n}\Lambda^{-1}\pi_{1}\circ\mathbf{S}_{n}\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{1}\rangle\circ p^{-1}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,p^{-1}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\rangle\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{n}\Lambda^{-1}\pi_{1}\circ p^{-1}\circ\langle\pi_{2},\pi_{1}\rangle\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,p^{-1}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\rangle\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{n}\text{app}\circ\mathbf{S}_{n}(\pi_{1}\times\text{id})\circ p^{-1}\circ\langle p^{-1}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\rangle,\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{n}\text{app}\circ p^{-1}\circ\mathbf{S}_{n}\pi_{1}\times\text{id}\circ\langle p^{-1}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\rangle,\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{n}\text{app}\circ p^{-1}\circ\mathbf{S}_{n}\pi_{1}\times\text{id}\circ p^{-1}\times\text{id}\circ\langle\langle\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\rangle,\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{n}\text{app}\circ p^{-1}\circ\pi_{1}\times\text{id}\circ p\times\text{id}\circ p^{-1}\times\text{id}\circ\langle\langle\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\rangle,\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{n}\text{app}\circ p^{-1}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{app}\circ\Lambda(\mathbf{S}_{n}\text{app}\circ p^{-1})\times\text{id}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket\rangle=\text{app}\circ\langle q\circ\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket\rangle=\llbracket\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket

•

\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}\equiv\mathbf{case}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\lambda\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b}\>(\mathbf{inj}_{1}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\>;\>\lambda\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b}\>(\mathbf{inj}_{2}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}})
.

Note that

\displaystyle\llbracket\lambda\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b}\>(\mathbf{inj}_{1}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\rrbracket

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\Lambda\Big(\text{app}\circ\langle q_{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket\circ\pi_{1},\mathbf{S}_{n}i_{1}\circ\pi_{2}\rangle\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\Lambda\Big(\text{app}\circ(\text{id}\times\mathbf{S}_{n}i_{1})\circ(q_{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket)\times\text{id}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\Lambda(\text{app}\circ(\text{id}\times\mathbf{S}_{n}i_{1}))\circ q_{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ(\mathbf{S}_{n}\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket)^{\mathbf{S}_{n}i_{1}}\circ q_{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{n}\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{i_{1}}\circ q^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ q_{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket}\circ\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket=\mathbf{S}_{n}\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{i_{1}}\circ\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket

Similarly,
\llbracket\lambda\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b}\>(\mathbf{inj}_{2}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\rrbracket=\mathbf{S}_{n}\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{i_{2}}\circ\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket
.

Now,

\displaystyle\llbracket\mathbf{case}\>\mathit{a}\>\mathbf{of}\>\lambda\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}.\mathit{b}\>(\mathbf{inj}_{1}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\>;\>\lambda\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}.\mathit{b}\>(\mathbf{inj}_{2}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\rrbracket

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{n}\text{app}\circ\mathbf{S}_{n}(\mathit{h}\times\text{id})\circ p^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket}\times\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket},\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}\circ p^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket},\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}}\times\text{id}\circ\langle\langle\mathbf{S}_{n}\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{i_{1}},\mathbf{S}_{n}\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{i_{1}}\rangle\circ\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{n}\text{app}\circ\mathbf{S}_{n}(\mathit{h}\times\text{id})\circ p^{-1}\circ p^{-1}\times\text{id}\circ\langle\mathbf{S}_{n}\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{i_{1}},\mathbf{S}_{n}\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{i_{1}}\rangle\times\text{id}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{n}\text{app}\circ\mathbf{S}_{n}(\mathit{h}\times\text{id})\circ p^{-1}\circ\mathbf{S}_{n}\langle\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{i_{1}},\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{i_{1}}\rangle\times\text{id}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{n}\text{app}\circ\mathbf{S}_{n}(\mathit{h}\times\text{id})\circ\mathbf{S}_{n}(\langle\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{i_{1}},\llbracket\mathit{B}\rrbracket^{i_{1}}\rangle\times\text{id})\circ p^{-1}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{n}(\text{app}\circ(\mathit{h}\times\text{id})\circ(h^{-1}\times\text{id}))\circ p^{-1}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{n}\text{app}\circ p^{-1}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket\rangle=\text{app}\circ\Lambda(\mathbf{S}_{n}\text{app}\circ p^{-1})\times\text{id}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket\rangle=\llbracket\mathit{b}\>\mathit{a}\rrbracket.

∎

Theorem H.2 (Theorem
10.2
).

Let
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{b}:^{0}\mathbf{Bool}
and
\mathit{v}
be a value of type
\mathbf{Bool}
. If
\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathit{v}\rrbracket
then
\vdash\mathit{b}\longmapsto^{\ast}\mathit{v}
.

Proof.

Let
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{b}:^{0}\mathbf{Bool}
and
\mathit{v}
be a boolean value such that
\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S})}=\llbracket\mathit{v}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S})}
.

We show that
\vdash\mathit{b}\longmapsto^{\ast}\mathit{v}
.

First note that
\lambda^{\circ}
is strongly normalizing with respect to the reduction relation,
\longmapsto^{\ast}
. Further,
\lambda^{\circ}
is also type sound with respect to this reduction relation.

Therefore, given
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{b}:^{0}\mathbf{Bool}
, we know that there exists a value
\mathit{v_{{\mathrm{0}}}}
such that
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{v_{{\mathrm{0}}}}:^{0}\mathbf{Bool}
and
\vdash\mathit{b}\longmapsto^{\ast}\mathit{v_{{\mathrm{0}}}}
.

Next, by Theorem
H.1
,
\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S})}=\llbracket\mathit{v_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S})}
.

Since
\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S})}=\llbracket\mathit{v}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S})}
(given) and
\llbracket\mathit{b}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S})}=\llbracket\mathit{v_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S})}
(above), therefore,
\llbracket\mathit{v}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S})}=\llbracket\mathit{v_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S})}
.

By injectivity,
\mathit{v}=\mathit{v_{{\mathrm{0}}}}
.

Thus,
\vdash\mathit{b}\longmapsto^{\ast}\mathit{v}
.
∎

Theorem H.3 (Theorem
10.3
).

\llbracket\_\rrbracket_{(\mathbf{Set},\mathbf{S}^{0})}
is a computationally adequate interpretation of
\lambda^{\circ}
.

Proof.

For a bicartesian category
\mathds{C}
, any strong monoidal functor from
\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{N})
to
\mathbf{End}_{\mathds{C}}^{\text{cc}}{}
provides a computationally adequate interpretation of
\lambda^{\circ}
, given the interpretation for ground types is injective. Now, with respect to
\llbracket\_\rrbracket_{(\mathbf{Set},\mathbf{S}^{0})}
, the interpretation for ground types is injective. As such, to prove that
(\mathbf{Set},\mathbf{S}^{0})
is a computationally adequate interpretation of
\lambda^{\circ}
, we just need to show that
\mathbf{S}^{0}
is a strong monoidal functor from
\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{N})
to
\mathbf{End}_{\mathds{C}}^{\text{cc}}{}
.

Recall the definition of
\mathbf{S}^{0}
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}^{0}(n)=\begin{cases}\textbf{Id}&\text{ if }n=0\\
\ast&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

By this definition,
\mathbf{S}^{0}({0})=\textbf{Id}{}
. Further,
\eta=\epsilon=\text{id}{}_{\textbf{Id}{}}
. Now for any
n_{{\mathrm{1}}},n_{{\mathrm{2}}}\in\mathbb{N}
, there are two cases to consider:

•

n_{{\mathrm{1}}}=n_{{\mathrm{2}}}=0
. Then
n_{{\mathrm{1}}}+n_{{\mathrm{2}}}=0
.

So,
\mathbf{S}^{0}({n_{{\mathrm{1}}}})=\mathbf{S}^{0}({n_{{\mathrm{2}}}})=\mathbf{S}^{0}({n_{{\mathrm{1}}}+n_{{\mathrm{2}}}})=\textbf{Id}{}
.

In this case,
\mu^{n_{{\mathrm{1}}},n_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=\delta^{n_{{\mathrm{1}}},n_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=\text{id}{}_{\textbf{Id}{}}
.

•

n_{{\mathrm{1}}}\neq 0
or
n_{{\mathrm{2}}}\neq 0
. Then,
n_{{\mathrm{1}}}+n_{{\mathrm{2}}}\neq 0
.

So,
\mathbf{S}^{0}({n_{{\mathrm{1}}}})=\ast
or
\mathbf{S}^{0}({n_{{\mathrm{2}}}})=\ast
. Hence,
\mathbf{S}^{0}({n_{{\mathrm{1}}}})\circ\mathbf{S}^{0}({n_{{\mathrm{2}}}})=\ast
.

Further,
\mathbf{S}^{0}({n_{{\mathrm{1}}}+n_{{\mathrm{2}}}})=\ast
.

In this case,
\mu^{n_{{\mathrm{1}}},n_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=\delta^{n_{{\mathrm{1}}},n_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=\text{id}{}_{\ast}
.

Hence,
\mathbf{S}^{0}
is a strong (in fact a strict) monoidal functor from
\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{N})
to
\mathbf{End}_{\mathds{C}}^{\text{cc}}{}
.
∎

Theorem H.4 (Theorem
10.4
).

Let
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{f}:^{0}\bigcirc\mathit{A}\to\mathbf{Bool}
and
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{0}\bigcirc\mathit{A}
and
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{0}\bigcirc\mathit{A}
. Then,
\vdash\mathit{f}\>\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\longmapsto^{\ast}\mathit{v}
if and only if
\vdash\mathit{f}\>\mathit{b_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\longmapsto^{\ast}\mathit{v}
, where
\mathit{v}
is a value of type
\mathbf{Bool}
.

Proof.

First note that
(\mathbf{Set},\mathbf{S}^{0})
gives us a computationally adequate model of
\lambda^{\circ}
(by Theorem
H.3
).

Next,
\llbracket\bigcirc\mathit{A}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{0})}=(\mathbf{S}^{0}({0^{\prime}}))\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{0})}=(\ast)\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket_{(\mathds{C},\mathbf{S}^{0})}=\top
.

Therefore,
\llbracket\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket_{(\mathbf{Set},\mathbf{S}^{0})}=\llbracket\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket_{(\mathbf{Set},\mathbf{S}^{0})}=\text{app}\circ\langle q\circ\llbracket\mathit{f}\rrbracket_{(\mathbf{Set},\mathbf{S}^{0})},\langle\rangle\rangle
.

Then, by Theorem
H.2
,
\vdash\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\longmapsto^{\ast}\mathit{v}
if and only if
\vdash\mathit{f}\>\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\longmapsto^{\ast}\mathit{v}
.
∎

Appendix I
Proofs of propositions/theorems stated in Section
11

Proposition I.1 (Proposition
11.1
).

Let
\mathcal{M}
be any preordered monoid. Then, in
\text{GMCC}_{\text{e}}
(
\mathcal{M}
),

•

The types
S_{m}\>\mathbf{Unit}
and
\mathbf{Unit}
are isomorphic.

•

The types
S_{m}\>(\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}})
and
S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
, for all types
\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
, are isomorphic.

•

The types
S_{m}\>(\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B})
and
S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\to S_{m}\>\mathit{B}
, for all types
\mathit{A}
and
\mathit{B}
, are isomorphic.

Proof.

For any two types,
\mathit{A}
and
\mathit{B}
, to show that
\mathit{A}\cong\mathit{B}
, we need to provide terms
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{f}:^{m^{\prime}}\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B}
and
\emptyset\vdash\mathit{g}:^{m^{\prime}}\mathit{B}\to\mathit{A}
such that
\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{g}\>(\mathit{f}\>\mathit{x})\equiv\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}\mathit{A}
and
\mathit{y}:^{m^{\prime}}\mathit{B}\vdash\mathit{f}\>(\mathit{g}\>\mathit{y})\equiv\mathit{y}:^{m^{\prime}}\mathit{B}
, for any
m^{\prime}\in M
. Note that the judgement
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\equiv\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{m}\mathit{A}
is a shorthand for the judgements
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{m}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{m}\mathit{A}
and
\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\equiv\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

•

Need to show:
S_{m}\>\mathbf{Unit}\cong\mathbf{Unit}
.

We have:

\emptyset\vdash\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathbf{unit}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathbf{Unit}\to\mathbf{Unit}\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathbf{Unit}\vdash\mathbf{unit}:^{m^{\prime}}\mathbf{Unit}\hskip 10.0pt\emptyset\vdash\lambda\mathit{y}.\mathbf{split}^{m}\mathbf{unit}:^{m^{\prime}}\mathbf{Unit}\to S_{m}\>\mathbf{Unit}\mathit{y}:^{m^{\prime}}\mathbf{Unit}\vdash\mathbf{split}^{m}\mathbf{unit}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathbf{Unit}\mathit{y}:^{m^{\prime}}\mathbf{Unit}\vdash\mathbf{unit}:^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathbf{Unit}

Say,
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\triangleq\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathbf{unit}
and
\mathit{g_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\triangleq\lambda\mathit{y}.\mathbf{split}^{m}\mathbf{unit}
.

Next,

\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathbf{Unit}\vdash\mathbf{unit}\equiv\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathbf{Unit}\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathbf{Unit}\vdash\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathbf{Unit}\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathbf{Unit}\vdash\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathbf{Unit}

So, given
\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathbf{Unit}
, we have,
\mathit{g_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>\mathit{x})\equiv\mathbf{split}^{m}\mathbf{unit}\equiv\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x})\equiv x
.

And, given
\mathit{y}:^{m^{\prime}}\mathbf{Unit}
, we have,
\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>(\mathit{g_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>\mathit{y})\equiv\mathbf{unit}\equiv\mathit{y}
.

•

Need to show:
S_{m}\>(\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\cong S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

We have:

\emptyset\vdash\lambda\mathit{x}.(\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x})),\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x}))):^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>(\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\to S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>(\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\vdash(\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x})),\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x}))):^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>(\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\vdash\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x})):^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>(\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\vdash\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x}):^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>(\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\vdash\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>(\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\vdash\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>(\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}})

and

\emptyset\vdash\lambda\mathit{y}.\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>\mathit{y}),\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>\mathit{y})):^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\to S_{m}\>(\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\mathit{y}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>\mathit{y}),\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>\mathit{y})):^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>(\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\mathit{y}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash(\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>\mathit{y}),\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>\mathit{y})):^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{y}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>\mathit{y}):^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{y}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>\mathit{y}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{y}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash\mathit{y}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}

Say,

\displaystyle\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}}

\displaystyle\triangleq\lambda\mathit{x}.(\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x})),\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x})))

\displaystyle\mathit{g_{{\mathrm{2}}}}

\displaystyle\triangleq\lambda\mathit{y}.\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>\mathit{y}),\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>\mathit{y}))

Next, given
\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>(\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}})
, we have,

\displaystyle\mathit{g_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{x})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle(\lambda\mathit{y}.\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>\mathit{y}),\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>\mathit{y})))

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt(\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x})),\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x})))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x}))),\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x}))))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x}),\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x}))\equiv\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x})\equiv x

And, given
\mathit{y}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times S_{m}\>\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
, we have,

\displaystyle\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>(\mathit{g_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{y})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle(\lambda\mathit{x}.(\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x})),\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x}))))

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt(\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>\mathit{y}),\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>\mathit{y})))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle(\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>\mathit{y}),\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>\mathit{y}))))),

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>\mathit{y}),\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>\mathit{y}))))))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle(\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>\mathit{y}),\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>\mathit{y}))),

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>\mathit{y}),\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>\mathit{y}))))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle(\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>\mathit{y})),\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>\mathit{y})))\equiv(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>\mathit{y},\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>\mathit{y})\equiv y

•

Need to show:
S_{m}\>(\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B})\cong S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\to S_{m}\>\mathit{B}
.

We have:

\mkern-25.0mu\emptyset\vdash\lambda\mathit{x}.\lambda\mathit{z}.\mathbf{split}^{m}((\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x})\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{z})):^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>(\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B})\to(S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\to S_{m}\>\mathit{B})\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>(\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B})\vdash\lambda\mathit{z}.\mathbf{split}^{m}((\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x})\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{z})):^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\to S_{m}\>\mathit{B}\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>(\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B}),\mathit{z}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\vdash\mathbf{split}^{m}((\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x})\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{z})):^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{B}\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>(\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B}),\mathit{z}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\vdash(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x})\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{z}):^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathit{B}\lx@proof@logical@and\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>(\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B}),\mathit{z}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\vdash\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B}\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>(\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B}),\mathit{z}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>(\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B})\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>(\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B}),\mathit{z}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\vdash\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{z}:^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathit{A}\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>(\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B}),\mathit{z}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{z}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}

and

\mkern-25.0mu\emptyset\vdash\lambda\mathit{y}.\mathbf{split}^{m}(\lambda\mathit{w}.\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathit{y}\>(\mathbf{split}^{m}\mathit{w}))):^{m^{\prime}}(S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\to S_{m}\>\mathit{B})\to S_{m}\>(\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B})\mathit{y}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\to S_{m}\>\mathit{B}\vdash\mathbf{split}^{m}(\lambda\mathit{w}.\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathit{y}\>(\mathbf{split}^{m}\mathit{w}))):^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>(\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B})\mathit{y}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\to S_{m}\>\mathit{B}\vdash\lambda\mathit{w}.\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathit{y}\>(\mathbf{split}^{m}\mathit{w})):^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B}\mathit{y}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\to S_{m}\>\mathit{B},\mathit{w}:^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathit{y}\>(\mathbf{split}^{m}\mathit{w})):^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathit{B}\mathit{y}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\to S_{m}\>\mathit{B},\mathit{w}:^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{y}\>(\mathbf{split}^{m}\mathit{w}):^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{B}\lx@proof@logical@and{\mathit{y}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\to S_{m}\>\mathit{B},\mathit{w}:^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{y}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\to S_{m}\>\mathit{B}}{\mathit{y}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\to S_{m}\>\mathit{B},\mathit{w}:^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathbf{split}^{m}\mathit{w}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\mathit{y}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\to S_{m}\>\mathit{B},\mathit{w}:^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{w}:^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathit{A}}

Say,

\displaystyle\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{3}}}}

\displaystyle\triangleq\lambda\mathit{x}.\lambda\mathit{z}.\mathbf{split}^{m}((\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x})\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{z}))

\displaystyle\mathit{g_{{\mathrm{3}}}}

\displaystyle\triangleq\lambda\mathit{y}.\mathbf{split}^{m}(\lambda\mathit{w}.\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathit{y}\>(\mathbf{split}^{m}\mathit{w})))

Next, given
\mathit{x}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>(\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B})
, we have,

\displaystyle\mathit{g_{{\mathrm{3}}}}\>(\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{3}}}}\>\mathit{x})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle(\lambda\mathit{y}.\mathbf{split}^{m}(\lambda\mathit{w}.\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathit{y}\>(\mathbf{split}^{m}\mathit{w}))))\>(\lambda\mathit{z}.\mathbf{split}^{m}((\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x})\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{z})))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{m}(\lambda\mathit{w}.\mathbf{merge}^{m}((\lambda\mathit{z}.\mathbf{split}^{m}((\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x})\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{z})))\>(\mathbf{split}^{m}\mathit{w})))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{m}(\lambda\mathit{w}.\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{split}^{m}((\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x})\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{split}^{m}\mathit{w})))))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{m}(\lambda\mathit{w}.\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{split}^{m}((\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x})\>\mathit{w})))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{m}(\lambda\mathit{w}.(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x})\>\mathit{w})\equiv\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x})\equiv x

And, given
\mathit{y}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\to S_{m}\>\mathit{B}
, we have,

\displaystyle\mathit{f_{{\mathrm{3}}}}\>(\mathit{g_{{\mathrm{3}}}}\>\mathit{y})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle(\lambda\mathit{x}.\lambda\mathit{z}.\mathbf{split}^{m}((\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{x})\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{z})))\>(\mathbf{split}^{m}(\lambda\mathit{w}.\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathit{y}\>(\mathbf{split}^{m}\mathit{w}))))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\lambda\mathit{z}.\mathbf{split}^{m}((\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{split}^{m}(\lambda\mathit{w}.\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathit{y}\>(\mathbf{split}^{m}\mathit{w})))))\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{z}))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\lambda\mathit{z}.\mathbf{split}^{m}((\lambda\mathit{w}.\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathit{y}\>(\mathbf{split}^{m}\mathit{w})))\>(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{z}))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\lambda\mathit{z}.\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathit{y}\>(\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{z}))))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\lambda\mathit{z}.\mathit{y}\>(\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{z}))\equiv\lambda\mathit{z}.\mathit{y}\>\mathit{z}\equiv y

∎

Theorem I.2 (Theorem
11.2
).

If
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{m}\mathit{A}
in
\text{GMCC}_{\text{e}}
, then
\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,\mathbf{S}_{m}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket)
. Further, if
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{m}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{m}\mathit{A}
such that
\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\equiv\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
in
\text{GMCC}_{\text{e}}
, then
\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,\mathbf{S}_{m}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket)
.

Proof.

The first part follows by induction on the typing derivation. Most of the cases are similar to those in Theorem
H.1
. We present the differing ones below.

•

Rule
E-Pair
. Have:
\Gamma\vdash(\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}):^{m}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
where
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{m}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{m}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

Need to show:
\llbracket(\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\rrbracket\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,\mathbf{S}_{m}(\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket\times\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket))
.

By IH,
\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,\mathbf{S}_{m}\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket)
and
\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,\mathbf{S}_{m}\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket)
.

Now,
\llbracket(\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\rrbracket=\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\xrightarrow{\langle\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\rangle}\mathbf{S}_{m}\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket\times\mathbf{S}_{m}\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\xrightarrow{p^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}}\mathbf{S}_{m}(\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket\times\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket)
.

•

Rule
E-Proj
. Have:
\Gamma\vdash\mathbf{proj}_{i}\>\mathit{a}:^{m}A_{i}
where
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{m}\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\times\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

Need to show:
\llbracket\mathbf{proj}_{i}\>\mathit{a}\rrbracket\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,\mathbf{S}_{m}\llbracket A_{i}\rrbracket)
.

By IH,
\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,\mathbf{S}_{m}(\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket\times\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket))
.

Now,
\llbracket\mathbf{proj}_{i}\>\mathit{a}\rrbracket=\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\xrightarrow{\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket}\mathbf{S}_{m}(\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket\times\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket)\xrightarrow{\mathbf{S}_{m}\pi_{i}}\mathbf{S}_{m}\llbracket A_{i}\rrbracket
.

•

Rule
E-Split
. Have:
\Gamma\vdash\mathbf{split}^{m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{a}:^{m_{{\mathrm{1}}}}S_{m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}
where
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{m_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cdot m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{A}
.

Need to show:
\llbracket\mathbf{split}^{m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{a}\rrbracket\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,\mathbf{S}_{m_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathbf{S}_{m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket)
.

By IH,
\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,\mathbf{S}_{m_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cdot m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket)
.

Now,
\llbracket\mathbf{split}^{m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{a}\rrbracket=\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\xrightarrow{\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket}\mathbf{S}_{m_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cdot m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket\xrightarrow{\delta^{m_{{\mathrm{1}}},m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}}\mathbf{S}_{m_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathbf{S}_{m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket
.

•

Rule
E-Merge
. Have:
\Gamma\vdash\mathbf{merge}^{m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{a}:^{m_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cdot m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{A}
where
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{m_{{\mathrm{1}}}}S_{m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}
.

Need to show:
\llbracket\mathbf{merge}^{m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{a}\rrbracket\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,\mathbf{S}_{m_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cdot m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket)
.

By IH,
\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,\mathbf{S}_{m_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathbf{S}_{m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket)
.

Now,
\llbracket\mathbf{merge}^{m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{a}\rrbracket=\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\xrightarrow{\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket}\mathbf{S}_{m_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathbf{S}_{m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket\xrightarrow{\mu^{m_{{\mathrm{1}}},m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}}\mathbf{S}_{m_{{\mathrm{1}}}\cdot m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket
.

•

Rule
E-Up
. Have:
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{A}
where
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{m_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{A}
and
m_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq m_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Need to show:
\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{A}\rrbracket\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,\mathbf{S}_{m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket)
.

By IH,
\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{m_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{A}\rrbracket\in\text{Hom}_{\mathds{C}}(\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket,\mathbf{S}_{m_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket)
.

Now,
\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{A}\rrbracket=\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket\xrightarrow{\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{m_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\mathbf{S}_{m_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket\xrightarrow{\mathbf{S}^{m_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}}\mathbf{S}_{m_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket
.

For the second part, invert the equality judgement,
\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\equiv\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
. Most of the cases are similar to those in Theorem
H.1
. We present the differing ones below.

•

\mathbf{proj}_{i}\>(\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\equiv a_{i}
.

Now,

\displaystyle\llbracket\mathbf{proj}_{i}\>(\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\rrbracket

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{m}\pi_{i}\circ p^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{i}\circ\langle\mathbf{S}_{m}\pi_{1},\mathbf{S}_{m}\pi_{2}\rangle\circ p^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{i}\circ p_{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}\circ p^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\rangle=\pi_{i}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket\rangle=\llbracket a_{i}\rrbracket.

•

\mathit{a}\equiv(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>\mathit{a},\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>\mathit{a})
.

Now,

\displaystyle\llbracket(\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>\mathit{a},\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>\mathit{a})\rrbracket

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}\circ\langle\llbracket\mathbf{proj}_{1}\>\mathit{a}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathbf{proj}_{2}\>\mathit{a}\rrbracket\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}\circ\langle\mathbf{S}_{m}\pi_{1}\circ\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket,\mathbf{S}_{m}\pi_{2}\circ\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}\circ\langle\mathbf{S}_{m}\pi_{1},\mathbf{S}_{m}\pi_{2}\rangle\circ\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket=p^{-1}_{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}\circ p_{\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathit{A_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\rrbracket}\circ\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathit{a}\rrbracket

•

\Gamma\vdash\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{split}^{m}\mathit{a})\equiv\mathit{a}:^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathit{A}
.

Now,

\displaystyle\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\mathbf{merge}^{m}(\mathbf{split}^{m}\mathit{a}):^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathit{A}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu^{m^{\prime},m}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\mathbf{split}^{m}\mathit{a}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu^{m^{\prime},m}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\delta^{m^{\prime},m}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathit{A}\rrbracket=\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathit{A}\rrbracket

•

\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}\equiv\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{a}):^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}
.

Now,

\displaystyle\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\mathbf{split}^{m}(\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{a}):^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta^{m^{\prime},m}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\mathbf{merge}^{m}\mathit{a}:^{m^{\prime}\cdot m}\mathit{A}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta^{m^{\prime},m}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\mu^{m^{\prime},m}_{\llbracket\mathit{A}\rrbracket}\circ\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\rrbracket=\llbracket\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{m^{\prime}}S_{m}\>\mathit{A}\rrbracket

∎

Theorem I.3 (Theorem
11.3
).

If
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:\mathit{A}
in GMCC(
\mathcal{L}
), then
\Gamma^{\bot}\vdash\widetilde{\mathit{a}}:^{\bot}\mathit{A}
in
\text{GMCC}_{\text{e}}
(
\mathcal{L}
). Further, if
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{A}
such that
\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\equiv\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
in GMCC(
\mathcal{L}
), then
\widetilde{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\equiv\widetilde{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}
in
\text{GMCC}_{\text{e}}
(
\mathcal{L}
).

Proof.

Let
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:\mathit{A}
in GMCC(
\mathcal{L}
). We show
\Gamma^{\bot}\vdash\widetilde{\mathit{a}}:^{\bot}\mathit{A}
in
\text{GMCC}_{\text{e}}
(
\mathcal{L}
) by induction on the typing derivation.

•

\lambda
-calculus. By IH.

•

Rule
MC-Return
. Have:
\Gamma\vdash\mathbf{ret}\>\mathit{a}:S_{\bot}\>\mathit{A}
where
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:\mathit{A}
.

Need to show:
\Gamma^{\bot}\vdash\mathbf{split}^{\bot}\widetilde{\mathit{a}}:^{\bot}S_{\bot}\>\mathit{A}
.

By IH,
\Gamma^{\bot}\vdash\widetilde{\mathit{a}}:^{\bot}\mathit{A}
.

This case follows by rule
E-Split
.

•

Rule
MC-Extract
. Have:
\Gamma\vdash\mathbf{extr}\>\mathit{a}:\mathit{A}
where
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:S_{\bot}\>\mathit{A}
.

Need to show:
\Gamma^{\bot}\vdash\mathbf{merge}^{\bot}\widetilde{\mathit{a}}:^{\bot}\mathit{A}
.

By IH,
\Gamma^{\bot}\vdash\widetilde{\mathit{a}}:^{\bot}S_{\bot}\>\mathit{A}
.

This case follows by rule
E-Merge
.

•

Rule
MC-Join
. Have:
\Gamma\vdash\mathbf{join}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{a}:S_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}
where
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:S_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>S_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}
.

Need to show:
\Gamma^{\bot}\vdash\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}})):^{\bot}S_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}
.

By IH,
\Gamma^{\bot}\vdash\widetilde{\mathit{a}}:^{\bot}S_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>S_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}
.

By rule
E-Merge
,
\Gamma^{\bot}\vdash\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}}:^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}S_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}
.

Applying rule
E-Merge
again,
\Gamma^{\bot}\vdash\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}}):^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{A}
.

This case, then, follows by rule
E-Split
.

•

Rule
MC-Fork
. Have:
\Gamma\vdash\mathbf{fork}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{a}:S_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>S_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}
where
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:S_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}
.

Need to show:
\Gamma^{\bot}\vdash\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}})):^{\bot}S_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>S_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}
.

By IH,
\Gamma^{\bot}\vdash\widetilde{\mathit{a}}:^{\bot}S_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}
.

By rule
E-Merge
,
\Gamma^{\bot}\vdash\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}}:^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{A}
.

By rule
E-Split
,
\Gamma^{\bot}\vdash\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}}):^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}S_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}
.

Applying rule
E-Split
again,
\Gamma^{\bot}\vdash\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}})):^{\bot}S_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>S_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}
.

•

Rule
MC-Fmap
. Have:
\Gamma\vdash\mathbf{lift}^{\ell}\mathit{f}:S_{\ell}\>\mathit{A}\to S_{\ell}\>\mathit{B}
where
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{f}:\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B}
.

Need to show:
\Gamma^{\bot}\vdash\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathbf{split}^{\ell}(\widetilde{\mathit{f}}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell}\mathit{x})):^{\bot}S_{\ell}\>\mathit{A}\to S_{\ell}\>\mathit{B}
.

By IH,
\Gamma^{\bot}\vdash\widetilde{\mathit{f}}:^{\bot}\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B}
.

Now,

\Gamma^{\bot}\vdash\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathbf{split}^{\ell}(\widetilde{\mathit{f}}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell}\mathit{x})):^{\bot}S_{\ell}\>\mathit{A}\to S_{\ell}\>\mathit{B}\Gamma^{\bot},\mathit{x}:^{\bot}S_{\ell}\>\mathit{A}\vdash\mathbf{split}^{\ell}(\widetilde{\mathit{f}}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell}\mathit{x})):^{\bot}S_{\ell}\>\mathit{B}\Gamma^{\bot},\mathit{x}:^{\bot}S_{\ell}\>\mathit{A}\vdash\widetilde{\mathit{f}}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell}\mathit{x}):^{\ell}\mathit{B}\lx@proof@logical@and\Gamma^{\bot},\mathit{x}:^{\bot}S_{\ell}\>\mathit{A}\vdash\widetilde{\mathit{f}}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B}\Gamma^{\bot},\mathit{x}:^{\bot}S_{\ell}\>\mathit{A}\vdash\widetilde{\mathit{f}}:^{\bot}\mathit{A}\to\mathit{B}\Gamma^{\bot},\mathit{x}:^{\bot}S_{\ell}\>\mathit{A}\vdash\mathbf{merge}^{\ell}\mathit{x}:^{\ell}\mathit{A}\Gamma^{\bot},\mathit{x}:^{\bot}S_{\ell}\>\mathit{A}\vdash\mathit{x}:^{\bot}S_{\ell}\>\mathit{A}

•

Rule
MC-Up
. Have:
\Gamma\vdash\mathbf{up}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{a}:S_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}
where
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:S_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>\mathit{A}
and
\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\sqsubseteq\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Need to show:
\Gamma^{\bot}\vdash\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}}):^{\bot}S_{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\>\mathit{A}
.

By IH,
\Gamma^{\bot}\vdash\widetilde{\mathit{a}}:^{\bot}S_{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\>\mathit{A}
.

By rule
E-Merge
,
\Gamma^{\bot}\vdash\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}}:^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{A}
.

By rule
E-Up
,
\Gamma^{\bot}\vdash\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}}:^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{A}
.

This case, then, follows by rule
E-Split
.

Next, we show that if
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{A}
such that
\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\equiv\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
in GMCC(
\mathcal{L}
), then
\widetilde{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\equiv\widetilde{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}
in
\text{GMCC}_{\text{e}}
(
\mathcal{L}
).

By inversion on
\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\equiv\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

•

\lambda
-calculus. By IH.

•

\mathbf{lift}^{\ell}(\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{x})\equiv\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{x}
.

Now,

\textstyle\mathbf{lift}^{\ell}(\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{x})

\textstyle\textstyle\mkern 0.2mu\sim

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\mathit{y}.\mathbf{split}^{\ell}((\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{x})\>(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell}\mathit{y}))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\lambda\mathit{y}.\mathbf{split}^{\ell}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell}\mathit{y})\equiv\lambda\mathit{y}.\mathit{y}

•

\mathbf{lift}^{\ell}(\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{g}\>(\mathit{f}\>\mathit{x}))\equiv\lambda\mathit{x}.(\mathbf{lift}^{\ell}\mathit{g})\>((\mathbf{lift}^{\ell}\mathit{f})\>\mathit{x})
.

Now,

\textstyle\mathbf{lift}^{\ell}(\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{g}\>(\mathit{f}\>\mathit{x}))

\textstyle\textstyle\mkern 0.2mu\sim

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\mathit{y}.\mathbf{split}^{\ell}((\lambda\mathit{x}.\widetilde{\mathit{g}}\>(\widetilde{\mathit{f}}\>\mathit{x}))\>(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell}\mathit{y}))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\lambda\mathit{y}.\mathbf{split}^{\ell}(\widetilde{\mathit{g}}\>(\widetilde{\mathit{f}}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell}\mathit{y})))

and

\textstyle\lambda\mathit{x}.(\mathbf{lift}^{\ell}\mathit{g})\>((\mathbf{lift}^{\ell}\mathit{f})\>\mathit{x})

\textstyle\textstyle\mkern 0.2mu\sim

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\lambda\mathit{x}.(\lambda\mathit{y}.\mathbf{split}^{\ell}(\widetilde{\mathit{g}}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell}\mathit{y})))\>(\mathbf{split}^{\ell}(\widetilde{\mathit{f}}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell}\mathit{x})))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathbf{split}^{\ell}(\widetilde{\mathit{g}}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell}(\mathbf{split}^{\ell}(\widetilde{\mathit{f}}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell}\mathit{x})))))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathbf{split}^{\ell}(\widetilde{\mathit{g}}\>(\widetilde{\mathit{f}}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell}\mathit{x})))

•

\mathbf{up}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{a}\equiv\mathit{a}
.

Now,
