\widetilde{\mathbf{up}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{a}}=\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}})\equiv\widetilde{\mathit{a}}
.

•

\mathbf{up}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{3}}}}(\mathbf{up}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{a})\equiv\mathbf{up}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{3}}}}\mathit{a}
.

Now,

\textstyle\mathbf{up}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{3}}}}(\mathbf{up}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{a})

\textstyle\textstyle\mkern 0.2mu\sim

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{3}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}})))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{3}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}})=\widetilde{\mathbf{up}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{3}}}}\mathit{a}}

•

(\mathbf{up}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{a})\>\mathchoice{\kern 7.75009pt\mathop{}\mathopen{\vphantom{\gg}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\ell^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}}}{\kern 7.75009pt\mathop{}\mathopen{\vphantom{\gg}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\ell^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}}}{\kern 10.96268pt\mathop{}\mathopen{\vphantom{\gg}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\ell^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}}}{\kern 12.42757pt\mathop{}\mathopen{\vphantom{\gg}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\ell^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}}}\gg\!\!=^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{f}\equiv\mathbf{up}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}},\ell^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathit{a}\>\mathchoice{\kern 7.75009pt\mathop{}\mathopen{\vphantom{\gg}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}}}{\kern 7.75009pt\mathop{}\mathopen{\vphantom{\gg}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}}}{\kern 10.96268pt\mathop{}\mathopen{\vphantom{\gg}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}}}{\kern 12.42757pt\mathop{}\mathopen{\vphantom{\gg}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}}}\gg\!\!=^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{f})
.

First, note that:

\textstyle\mathit{a}\>\mathchoice{\kern 7.75009pt\mathop{}\mathopen{\vphantom{\gg}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}}}{\kern 7.75009pt\mathop{}\mathopen{\vphantom{\gg}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}}}{\kern 10.96268pt\mathop{}\mathopen{\vphantom{\gg}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}}}{\kern 12.42757pt\mathop{}\mathopen{\vphantom{\gg}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}}}\gg\!\!=^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{f}

\textstyle\textstyle\mkern 0.2mu\sim

\displaystyle=

\textstyle\mathbf{join}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}((\mathbf{lift}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{f})\>\mathit{a})

\textstyle\textstyle\mkern 0.2mu\sim

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{lift}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{f}}\>\widetilde{\mathit{a}})))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(\widetilde{\mathit{f}}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}})))))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\widetilde{\mathit{f}}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}})))

Next,

\textstyle(\mathbf{up}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{a})\>\mathchoice{\kern 7.75009pt\mathop{}\mathopen{\vphantom{\gg}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\ell^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}}}{\kern 7.75009pt\mathop{}\mathopen{\vphantom{\gg}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\ell^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}}}{\kern 10.96268pt\mathop{}\mathopen{\vphantom{\gg}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\ell^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}}}{\kern 12.42757pt\mathop{}\mathopen{\vphantom{\gg}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\ell^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}}}\gg\!\!=^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{f}

\textstyle\textstyle\mkern 0.2mu\sim

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{\ell^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\widetilde{\mathit{f}}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\widetilde{\mathbf{up}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{a}})))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{\ell^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\widetilde{\mathit{f}}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(\mathbf{split}^{\ell^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}})))))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{\ell^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\widetilde{\mathit{f}}\>(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}})))

and

\textstyle\mathbf{up}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}},\ell^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathit{a}\>\mathchoice{\kern 7.75009pt\mathop{}\mathopen{\vphantom{\gg}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}}}{\kern 7.75009pt\mathop{}\mathopen{\vphantom{\gg}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}}}{\kern 10.96268pt\mathop{}\mathopen{\vphantom{\gg}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}}}{\kern 12.42757pt\mathop{}\mathopen{\vphantom{\gg}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}}}\gg\!\!=^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{f})

\textstyle\textstyle\mkern 0.2mu\sim

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{\ell^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 88.4404pt\hskip-88.4404pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathit{a}\>\mathchoice{\kern 7.75009pt\mathop{}\mathopen{\vphantom{\gg}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}}}{\kern 0.2mu\sim$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{3700883}{1272907}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\textstyle\mkern 0.2mu\sim$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{4326816}{1272907}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptstyle\mkern 0.2mu\sim$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{5334030}{1272907}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.2mu\sim$}}}}}$}}}}}}\hskip-230.50055pt\hskip 17.76364pt}}=\mathbf{merge}^{\bot}(\mathbf{split}^{\bot}\widetilde{\mathit{a}})\equiv\widetilde{\mathit{a}}
.

•

\mathbf{join}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{fork}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{a})\equiv\mathit{a}
.

Now,

\textstyle\mathbf{join}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{fork}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{a})

\textstyle\textstyle\mkern 0.2mu\sim

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\widetilde{\mathbf{fork}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{a}}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}})))))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}})))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}})\equiv\widetilde{\mathit{a}}

•

\mathbf{fork}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{join}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{a})\equiv\mathit{a}
.

Now,

\textstyle\mathbf{fork}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{join}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{a})

\textstyle\textstyle\mkern 0.2mu\sim

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\widetilde{\mathbf{join}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{a}}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vee\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}})))))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{2}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}})))

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathbf{split}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(\mathbf{merge}^{\ell_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\widetilde{\mathit{a}})\equiv\widetilde{\mathit{a}}

∎

Theorem I.4 (Theorem
11.4
).

If
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a}:^{n}\mathit{A}
in
\lambda^{\circ}
, then
\widehat{\Gamma}\vdash\widehat{\mathit{a}}:^{n}\widehat{\mathit{A}}
in
\text{GMCC}_{\text{e}}
(
\mathcal{N}
). Further, if
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:^{n}\mathit{A}
and
\Gamma\vdash\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:^{n}\mathit{A}
such that
\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\equiv\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
in
\lambda^{\circ}
, then
\widehat{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}\equiv\widehat{\mathit{a_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}
in
\text{GMCC}_{\text{e}}
(
\mathcal{N}
).

Proof.

The first part follows by induction on the typing derivation. The second part follows by inversion on the equality judgement.
∎

Appendix J
Proof of proposition mentioned in Section
12

Proposition J.1.

The exponential object in
\mathcal{DC}
does not satisfy the universal property, unless the relations in the definition of
\text{Obj}(\mathcal{DC})
are restricted to reflexive ones only.

Proof.

An object of
\mathcal{DC}
is a pair,
X:=(|X|,R_{X,\ell})
, where
|X|
is a set and
R_{X,\ell}
is a family of binary relations on
|X|
, indexed by elements,
\ell
, of the parametrizing lattice,
\mathcal{L}
. (Strictly speaking, an object is a pair consisting of a cpo and a family of directed-complete relations. However, since we are not considering nonterminating computations, we can simplify it to a set and a family of relations.) A morphism from
X
to
Y
is any function from
|X|
to
|Y|
that respects the binary relations, i.e. a function
h:|X|\to|Y|
such that if
(x_{1},x_{2})\in R_{X,\ell}
, then
(h\>x_{1},h\>x_{2})\in R_{Y,\ell}
.

A product object is defined in
\mathcal{DC}
as follows:

\mathit{X}\times\mathit{Y}:=(|X|\times|Y|,\;\{((x_{1},y_{1}),(x_{2},y_{2}))\>|\>(x_{1},x_{2})\in R_{X,\ell}\>\wedge\>(y_{1},y_{2})\in R_{Y,\ell}\}

An exponential object is defined as:

\mathit{X}\Rightarrow\mathit{Y}:=(\text{Hom}_{\mathcal{DC}}(X,Y),\;\{(f,g)\>|\>\forall(x_{1},x_{2})\in R_{X,\ell},(f\>x_{1},g\>x_{2})\in R_{Y,\ell}\})

Now we present our counter-example. Let the parametrizing lattice
\mathcal{L}
be
\mathbf{Public}\sqsubset\mathbf{Secret}
. For a set
A
, let
id
and
true
denote the identity relation on
A
and the total relation on
A
respectively. Now define the following objects in
\mathcal{DC}
:

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle:=(\{x_{1},x_{2}\},\>R_{X,\mathbf{Public}}=R_{X,\mathbf{Secret}}=\{(x_{1},x_{1})\})

\displaystyle Y

\displaystyle:=(\{y_{1},y_{2}\},\>R_{Y,\mathbf{Public}}=\textit{true}\>\wedge\>R_{Y,\mathbf{Secret}}=\textit{id})

\displaystyle Z

\displaystyle:=(\{z_{1},z_{2}\},\>R_{Z,\mathbf{Public}}=R_{Z,\mathbf{Secret}}=\textit{id})

Then,
\text{Hom}_{\mathcal{DC}}(\mathit{X}\times\mathit{Y},Z)
has
8
elements but
\text{Hom}_{\mathcal{DC}}(X,\mathit{Y}\Rightarrow\mathit{Z})
has only
4
elements. Therefore,
\mathit{Y}\Rightarrow\mathit{Z}
does not satisfy the universal property. Hence, category
\mathcal{DC}
, as presented by
Abadi
et al. 1999
, is not cartesian closed.

An analysis of the above counter-example shows that the problem stems from the fact that
R_{X,\ell}
does not relate
x_{2}
to itself. In fact, when we set
R_{X,\mathbf{Public}}=R_{X,\mathbf{Secret}}=\textit{id}
, both
\text{Hom}_{\mathcal{DC}}(\mathit{X}\times\mathit{Y},Z)
and
\text{Hom}_{\mathcal{DC}}(X,\mathit{Y}\Rightarrow\mathit{Z})
have
4
elements. Below, we show that if for every object
X
, the relation
R_{X,\ell}
is required to be reflexive, then the exponential object indeed satisfies the universal property.

Let
X,Y,Z\in\text{Obj}(\mathcal{DC})
. Define the exponential object,
\mathit{X}\Rightarrow\mathit{Y}
, as above. Need to check that for any
h\in\text{Hom}_{\mathcal{DC}}(X,Y)
, we have,
(h,h)\in R_{\mathit{X}\Rightarrow\mathit{Y},\ell}
.

Suppose,
(\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\in R_{X,\ell}
. Need to show:
(\mathit{h}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{h}\>\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\in R_{Y,\ell}
.

But this is true because
h\in\text{Hom}_{\mathcal{DC}}(X,Y)
.

Next, we define
app
as:

(\mathit{X}\Rightarrow\mathit{Y})\times\mathit{X}\xrightarrow{\mathbf{\lambda}w.(\pi_{1}\>\mathit{w})\>(\pi_{2}\>\mathit{w})}Y

Need to check that
app
is a
\mathcal{DC}
-morphism.

Suppose,
(\mathit{w},\mathit{w^{\prime}})\in R_{(\mathit{X}\Rightarrow\mathit{Y})\times\mathit{X},\ell}
. Then
(\pi_{1}\>\mathit{w},\pi_{1}\>\mathit{w^{\prime}})\in R_{\mathit{X}\Rightarrow\mathit{Y},\ell}
and
(\pi_{2}\>\mathit{w},\pi_{2}\>\mathit{w^{\prime}})\in R_{X,\ell}
.

Therefore,
((\pi_{1}\>\mathit{w})\>(\pi_{2}\>\mathit{w}),(\pi_{1}\>\mathit{w^{\prime}})\>(\pi_{2}\>\mathit{w^{\prime}}))\in R_{Y,\ell}
.

Now, say
h\in\text{Hom}_{\mathcal{DC}}(\mathit{Z}\times\mathit{X},Y)
. We define
\Lambda\mathit{h}\in\text{Hom}_{\mathcal{DC}}(Z,\mathit{X}\Rightarrow\mathit{Y})
as:

\Lambda\mathit{h}\triangleq\mathbf{\lambda}z.\mathbf{\lambda}x.\mathit{h}\>(\mathit{z},\mathit{x})

Need to check the following:

•

First that
\Lambda\mathit{h}
is well-defined.

For any
\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\in|Z|
, need to show:
(\Lambda\mathit{h})\>\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{0}}}}\in|\mathit{X}\Rightarrow\mathit{Y}|
, i.e.
\mathbf{\lambda}x.\mathit{h}\>(\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{0}}}},\mathit{x})\in\text{Hom}_{\mathcal{DC}}(X,Y)
.

Suppose
(\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\in R_{X,\ell}
. Need to show:
(\mathit{h}\>(\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{0}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}),\mathit{h}\>(\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{0}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}))\in R_{Y,\ell}
.

Since
R_{Z,\ell}
is reflexive
,
(\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{0}}}},\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{0}}}})\in R_{Z,\ell}
. Therefore,
((\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{0}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}),(\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{0}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}))\in R_{\mathit{Z}\times\mathit{X},\ell}
.

This implies that
(\mathit{h}\>(\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{0}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}),\mathit{h}\>(\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{0}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}))\in R_{Y,\ell}
[
\because h\in\text{Hom}_{\mathcal{DC}}(\mathit{Z}\times\mathit{X},Y)
].

•

Next, that
\Lambda\mathit{h}
is a
\mathcal{DC}
-morphism.

For
(\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\in R_{Z,\ell}
, need to show
(\mathbf{\lambda}x.\mathit{h}\>(\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x}),\mathbf{\lambda}x.\mathit{h}\>(\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\mathit{x}))\in R_{\mathit{X}\Rightarrow\mathit{Y},\ell}
.

Suppose
(\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\in R_{X,\ell}
. Need to show:
(\mathit{h}\>(\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}),\mathit{h}\>(\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}))\in R_{Y,\ell}
.

From what we are given,
((\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}),(\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}))\in R_{\mathit{Z}\times\mathit{X},\ell}
.

As such,
(\mathit{h}\>(\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}),\mathit{h}\>(\mathit{z_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{2}}}}))\in R_{Y,\ell}
[
\because h\in\text{Hom}_{\mathcal{DC}}(\mathit{Z}\times\mathit{X},Y)
].

Now we check that
\Lambda\mathit{h}
satisfies the standard existence and uniqueness properties:

•

Existence.

\displaystyle\text{app}\circ(\Lambda\mathit{h}\times\text{id})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{\lambda}\mathit{v}.\text{app}\>(\Lambda\mathit{h}\>(\pi_{1}\>\mathit{v}),\pi_{2}\>\mathit{v})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{\lambda}\mathit{v}.(\mathbf{\lambda}\mathit{w}.(\pi_{1}\>\mathit{w})\>(\pi_{2}\>\mathit{w}))\>((\mathbf{\lambda}\mathit{z}.\mathbf{\lambda}\mathit{x}.\mathit{h}\>(\mathit{z},\mathit{x}))\>(\pi_{1}\>\mathit{v}),\pi_{2}\>\mathit{v})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{\lambda}\mathit{v}.(\mathbf{\lambda}\mathit{z}.\mathbf{\lambda}\mathit{x}.\mathit{h}\>(\mathit{z},\mathit{x}))\>(\pi_{1}\>\mathit{v})\>(\pi_{2}\>\mathit{v})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{\lambda}\mathit{v}.\mathit{h}\>(\pi_{1}\>\mathit{v},\pi_{2}\>\mathit{v})=\mathbf{\lambda}\mathit{v}.\mathit{h}\>\mathit{v}=\mathit{h}

•

Uniqueness.

Suppose,
h^{\prime}\in\text{Hom}_{\mathcal{DC}}(Z,\mathit{X}\Rightarrow\mathit{Y})
such that
\text{app}\circ(\mathit{h^{\prime}}\times\text{id})=h
.

Then,

\mathit{h}\>(\mathit{z},\mathit{x})=(\text{app}\circ\mathit{h^{\prime}}\times\text{id})\>(\mathit{z},\mathit{x})=(\mathbf{\lambda}\mathit{w}.(\pi_{1}\>\mathit{w})\>(\pi_{2}\>\mathit{w}))\>(\mathit{h^{\prime}}\>\mathit{z},\mathit{x})=\mathit{h^{\prime}}\>\mathit{z}\>\mathit{x}

But,
\mathit{h}\>(\mathit{z},\mathit{x})=\Lambda\mathit{h}\>\mathit{z}\>\mathit{x}
.

So, by function extensionality,
\mathit{h^{\prime}}=\Lambda\mathit{h}
.

∎
